FILE ~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\README.md
1: # Full FEI: 100-agent plan and twenty scheduled research streams
2: 
3: **Full FEI remains OPEN. Campaign deadline: 21 October 2026, 22:15:57 Asia/Jerusalem (19:15:57 UTC).**
4: 
5: [Research plan (PDF)](FEI_100_agent_research_plan.pdf) · [LaTeX source](FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex) · [100 assignment contracts](AGENT_ASSIGNMENTS.json) · [Execution protocol](EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.json) · [Current status](CURRENT_STATUS.md)
6: 
7: The report describes 100 logical research assignments in twenty squads of five. The twenty existing account01/B01–B20 hourly tasks execute those backlogs one focused packet at a time. The allocation does not claim that 100 concurrent workers have been launched. The original recurrence phases and finite counts remain in force; the campaign clock is not restarted by a report revision.
8: 
9: ## Research and execution
10: 
11: The main objectives are a complete expected whole-set witness cost and a nonlocal physical restriction payment. Signed Booleanity, conditional phase entropy, arbitrary weighted thresholds under product biases, and low-energy/shared-variable composition provide distinct routes and substantial class targets. All constants, normalizers, rare-event logarithms and Boolean realizability requirements remain explicit.
12: 
13: The independently audited revision removes targets already proved, adds known counterexamples with their exact scopes, and gives every A-packet a concrete deliverable, acceptance test, bounded initial cycle, intended consumers and independent reviewers. Baseline imports finish once; subsequent runs advance their next unresolved implication. Three stagnant substantive attempts on the same stable gap prompt a change of method or a justified narrower theorem.
14: 
15: - [Packet coverage](PACKET_STATUS.json) separates artifact completion from mathematical resolution.
16: - [Claims and stable gaps](CLAIM_REGISTRY.json) point to exact source versions and review records.
17: - [Obstruction registry](OBSTRUCTION_REGISTRY.json) preserves the failed statements and valid scope of each control.
18: - B20 maintains the concise indices and coverage of all hundred packets. Original derivations remain in immutable run folders.
26: ## Observed work
27: 
28: B05 has produced a rigorous local-refund no-go, independently reconstructed by B19. These satisfy the first matching deliverables of A025 and A092. The B09 biased-restriction report is saved and awaiting independent review, with explicit sharpness and composition-scope questions. See the original source pins in `CLAIM_REGISTRY.json`; a scheduled timestamp alone is not proof of completed mathematical work.
29: 
30: ## Public paper and private report
31: 
46: The later public data-availability-only edit at `b38ba0ab49618ccbe6d1518a34c141dcec9f247c` is preserved; the report's historical mathematical citations remain valid source pins. The corrected paper PDF and source were fetched and matched to their exact current hashes.
47: 
48: The concurrent B09 certificate repair found a signed interval-division error in the original logarithm checker. The repaired rational calculation independently certifies the narrow two-bit margin `J_A-M_A > 0.1195` bits. Its report, code, output and claims are preserved at `verification/B09_interval_repair_20261011/`. This is an interactive repair, not another natural scheduled run and not acceptance of B09's full theorem or endpoint/composition claims.

4: 
5: ## Completed paper and recovery
6: 
7: The finished paper has 38 pages and is public at [b38ba0ab4961](https://github.com/yspennstate/fourier-entropy-threshold-functions/commit/b38ba0ab49618ccbe6d1518a34c141dcec9f247c). All twenty scientific files were verified against the completed package. Its main regular/all-LTF bound is `H <= C_reg D`, with `C_reg = log2(4+e) + 2 log2(e*pi) < 8.937`. The shared-data extension covers fresh physical selectors and orthogonal neighboring branches, with the already proved positive-selector-energy corollary. Full FEI is not claimed.
8:

No pending repo-creation or upload work remains.

18: 
19: ## Current mathematical frontier
20: 
21: 1. **G-J-COST:** pay the complete conditioned posterior witness expectation, including root normalization, labels, ranks, rarity and latent-witness entropy.
22: 2. **G-R-SELECT / G-R-MISMATCH:** choose actual coordinates/proper blocks or a stopping tree and pay mismatch logarithms minus KL credit plus terminal entropy without reused influence.
23: 3. **G-BOOLEANITY / G-PHASE-COND:** derive a full-Booleanity consequence beyond refuted fixed prefixes, retaining the unpaid conditional spectral entropy.
24: 4. **G-BIASED-LTF:** prove a corrected product-biased weighted-threshold result surviving the three known physical-TC/pivotal-credit controls.
25: 5. **G-LOWSEL-COLLISION:** go beyond the already proved positive-energy selector corollary to a new unbounded family, low-energy collision payment or explicitly controlled reused selectors.
41: The nine-file B09 bundle is at open-problems commit `adb671ffcda143fa68347806bb96e8c305621a7d`, run `runs/B09/20261011T195500Z-account01-B09-biased-restriction-uncertainty/`; Brain mirror `bfe8eafa2194feb32cb3881957ef844b8fe30de0`. Its original report was read. It authors a biased physical-block uncertainty lemma and a rational-interval two-bit AND counterexample to a tax-free helper.
42: 
43: Independent review has not yet accepted it. In particular, section 5(4) cannot infer impossibility of every O(p) replacement solely from the asymptotic of an available upper-bound tax; that needs a lower-bound family or a narrower claim. Exact tensor additivity must also be distinguished from broader composition. B09/B19 should review the core lemma, fixed certificate and stronger consequences separately. This source is not an accepted premise for a full-FEI argument.

54: The constant must be independent of dimension, arity, depth, overlap, probability of the minority output, and any unbounded representation parameter. An explicit large constant would settle the conjecture. Optimizing that constant is secondary to proving its finiteness.
55: 
56: Throughout this report, entropy is in bits. Some historical Brain notes use natural logarithms and raw entropy of indicators. A coefficient in such a note cannot be copied into a sign-function statement without the corresponding conversion. Every imported result must state its measure, Fourier basis, normalization, log base, and treatment of the empty coefficient.
57: 
58: \subsection{Recovered manuscripts and verified scope}
59: 
60: The interrupted work supplied a 36-page version of \emph{Fourier entropy of threshold functions and their compositions}. The audited recovery and shared-data extension produce the 38-page public paper, released at repository commit \texttt{cd27aa96a5bb670a5b3ae5451095b7020db8e9d4} \cite{Threshold}. Its principal constant is
61: \[
62:  \Creg=\log_2(4+e)+2\log_2(e\pi)
63:        =8.93647465787758\ldots.
64: \]
65: With $D(f)=\sum_i\min\{\I_i(f),1-\I_i(f)\}$, its Theorem A gives $\HH(f)\le\Creg D(f)$ for every regular Boolean function. Here regular means unate with a total desirability order, and therefore includes every deterministic linear threshold function with arbitrary real weights. It does not mean small or uniformly comparable weights.
66: 
67: The proof proceeds through pivotal antichains, a relative resampling estimate, harmonic control of spectral ranks, and an Euler-product reference on Fourier levels. The proof was reconstructed in an independent audit during recovery; the product-biased threshold and bounded-overlap arguments were also independently read. No gap was identified in those audits. These are mathematical checks of a preprint, not external peer review or proof-assistant certification.
68: 
75: \subsection{Why the literature check matters}
76: 
77: The corrected Chakraborty--Kulkarni--Lokam--Saurabh work bounds LTF entropy by $O(\sqrt n)$; an earlier all-LTF claim was removed. The random-LTF result treats specified random models \cite{CKLS,RandomLTF}. This makes verification of the recovered deterministic theorem particularly important. The current argument must stand on its complete proof, independently of earlier abstracts or claims.
78: 
79: Existing coding and composition theorems are essential prerequisites, especially Wan--Wright--Wu and O'Donnell--Tan \cite{WWW,ODT}. Shalev's low-influence reductions explain why rarity alone is not a structural simplification \cite{Shalev}. Recent restriction work supplies a directly relevant stopping-tree framework \cite{GMP}; the plateaued-function theorem supplies a useful structured benchmark \cite{Potapov}. The author's percolation result supplies a geometric encoding example, with spatial hypotheses that must remain explicit \cite{Percolation}.
80: 
81: Do not spend the campaign rediscovering the generic coordinate-marginal entropy estimate as a Boolean breakthrough. Keller--Mossel--Schlank already give the relevant general estimate \cite{KMS}. A later preprint claiming an information-theoretic sharpening was withdrawn because the result already appeared there \cite{Withdrawn}. A new universal argument must use additional structure of actual Boolean spectra or a genuinely stronger global accounting mechanism.
82: 
186: $\HH(f)$ itself; it is not a reduction in difficulty.
187: 
188: \paragraph{A sharper existing mismatch/KL bound.}
189: The recovered mismatch/KL estimate is a sharper starting point for
190: restriction work than the scalar profile alone \cite{BrainMismatch}.
191: For the actual Boolean children $g=f_i^+$ and $h=f_i^-$, put
192: \[
193:  a_T=\wh g(T),\quad b_T=\wh h(T),\quad
221: together with terminal entropy. Bounding this complete quantity
222: by a universal multiple of root influence remains open.
223: The mismatch mass $\mu_i^{\rm mis}$ is different from the
224: influence-variation refund denoted $M$ in the B05 report.
225: 
226: \paragraph{A newly verified local refund obstruction.}
239: \]
240: These two refunds differ from the mixing term $M_i$ and the
241: mismatch mass $\mu_i^{\rm mis}$ above.
242: The first natural B05 run supplies a further actual Boolean
243: control. For even $k\ge4$, use the quadratic bent sign on $k$
244: bits, its one-point flip, and activate both on one point of
300: Fixed prefixes are already known to be insufficient in two
301: distinct relaxations. The archived signed-convolution-order
302: family is normalized and dyadic and passes every prescribed
303: fixed cancellation order while its erasure/influence ratio
304: diverges \cite{BrainBooleanOrder}. A separate cyclic construction
305: passes every fixed atom-cap prefix while retaining an unbounded
312: \subsection{Arbitrary biased weighted thresholds}\label{sec:weighted-guards}
313: 
314: The public paper supplies the correct product-measure strong entropy normalization and proves it for symmetric thresholds. Extending this to arbitrary weighted thresholds with a constant uniform in weights, biases, and arity would immediately enlarge the depth-independent formula class through the established composition principle \cite{ODT,Threshold}. This is a substantial secondary target even if full FEI remains open.
315: 
316: For clarity, in the orthonormal product Fourier basis with
317: $v_i=4q_i(1-q_i)$ the target normalization is
318: \[
319:  \HH_\mu^+(f)=\sum_{S\ne\varnothing}\wh f_\mu(S)^2
320:        \log_2\frac{\prod_{i\in S}v_i}{\wh f_\mu(S)^2},
323: \]
324: The symmetric proof uses integer-valued sums, exponential
325: tilting, a mode estimate and a near-dictator regime. A weighted
326: extension needs a replacement that survives the already proved
327: obstructions below; a grid of biases cannot certify the
328: continuum or endpoint statement.
329: 
330: The separate reductions manuscript already supplies three
331: normalization obstructions that every proposed extension must
332: respect \cite{Reductions}. One is the weighted threshold
333: \[

372: \subsection{Shared-data composition}
373: 
374: For uniform physical selector bits $x\in\bits^a$ disjoint from the data cube and arbitrary Boolean branches $g_b$ on that common data cube, let $F(x,y)=g_x(y)$. With $N=2^a$, set
375: \[
377:  r_T(b)=\frac{\wh g_b(T)^2}{NR_T}.
378: \]
379: For $R_T>0$, let $\nu_T$ be the squared normalized Walsh transform of the signed vector $\wh g_b(T)/\sqrt{NR_T}$. Omit zero-mass terms. Then
380: \[
381:  H(F)=a+N^{-1}\sum_bH(g_b)
382:        +\sum_T R_T[H(\nu_T)-H(r_T)].
436: Boolean cyclic phase realization & Paying a marginal may force auxiliary energy yet leave conditional entropy. & State and pay the conditional or joint remainder explicitly. \\
437: Coherent near-tensor surgery & A remainder can contain $\alpha^2\log(1/\alpha^2)$. & Prove an amortized logarithmic payment; $O(\alpha^2)$ alone is false. \\
438: Bias endpoints and near dictators & Corrected entropy and $\I-\Var$ can simultaneously degenerate. & Check limiting ratios and normalization credit analytically. \\
439: Bent selector splice & A selected one-bit increment divided by selector influence can diverge. & Do not assert an all-coordinate or selector-only constant; preserve existential scope. \\
440: B05 rare refunds & Fixed local prices on $d,V_{\rm post},M_{\rm inf}$ miss the rare logarithm. & Analyze a chosen complete tree or a nonlocal logarithmic credit. \\
441: Weighted threshold: global TC & Conditional physical $\alpha\operatorname{TC}/\I$ grows with arity. & Prove a replacement normalization that survives Section~\ref{sec:weighted-guards}. \\
442: Weighted threshold: pivotal credit & Coefficient-one pivotal logarithms leave an unbounded residual. & Keep the exact coefficient scope; larger multipliers are not refuted. \\
443: Weighted threshold: local TC & Every coordinate can fail the conditional physical TC payment. & Do not identify this failed target with spectral good-coordinate erasure. \\
444: Fixed convolution or cap prefixes & Distinct non-Boolean families pass every prescribed fixed prefix. & Name the hierarchy and supply an additional full-Booleanity consequence. \\
445: Raw convolution tensors & Raw $L$ can grow exponentially while influence is additive. & Retain normalized $T=H(v/Z)-\log_2Z$ and its still-unpaid bound. \\
446: Overlapping Boolean products & $H(fg)\le H(f)+H(g)$ is false when variables are shared. & Use an exact collision ledger; disjoint additivity remains valid. \\

1279: When a claim version is refuted, B20 flags its dependent conclusions as \texttt{BLOCKED\_DEPENDENCY} or \texttt{CONDITIONAL}, preserves the original witness, and routes the correction to the named consumers. A consumer records the actual repaired substitution in its next substantive report. Delivery receipts are not evidence that a correction has been used in a proof. The registry organizes evidence; it cannot certify a theorem by changing a status field.
1280: 
1281: At each invocation B20 checks coverage of all hundred A-packets and all report elements: exact endpoints, representation assumptions, normalization, mandatory counterexamples, computations, reviews, class consequences and final handoff. It identifies unattended high-priority work and assigns the nearest ready step within existing capacity. Its own run must also contain a substantive synthesis or derivation. The aim is to finish every useful deliverable while retaining an accurate list of mathematically unresolved targets.

FILE ~\mop_fei100\research_pushes\2026-10-11-full-fei-100-agents\CAMPAIGN.md
1: # Full FEI: mathematical supplement to the installed campaign
2: 
3: **Status: SUPPLEMENT. Full FEI remains OPEN.** This directory adds mathematical routes, source notes, counterexamples, and proof interfaces to the installed plan in [reports/fei-2026-10-11/](https://github.com/yspennstate/math-open-problems/tree/master/reports/fei-2026-10-11/). The installation reference is [commit bc85302284612e979faae67ea51663242d322221](https://github.com/yspennstate/math-open-problems/commit/bc85302284612e979faae67ea51663242d322221). The installed report and subsequent authorized updates remain the primary campaign instructions.
4: 
5: **Fixed deadline: 21 October 2026, 19:15:57 UTC (2026-10-21T19:15:57Z).** This supplement uses the installed endpoint. It does not reset the ten-day period, restart a recurrence, alter an existing COUNT, or extend the final eligible run.
6: 
7: **Live identities: B01–B20.** Their installed assignments are preserved in [NATIVE_MAPPING.md](NATIVE_MAPPING.md). The F01–F20 labels in this directory are research role groups in a proposed allocation of 100 logical roles, five per group. They are topical labels, not replacement native task identities. Numerical suffixes do not establish a B-to-F assignment. One B stream may use several F groups, and one F group may serve several B streams.
8: 
9: ## How to use the two reports together
10: 
11: Start with the installed report and the existing stream's own latest verified state. Consult this supplement for a specific mathematical obligation, source, or obstruction relevant to that B stream. Use [NATIVE_MAPPING.md](NATIVE_MAPPING.md) to find appropriate F-role groups; its associations are advisory research connections, not a reassignment.
12: 
13: The supporting files are:
14: 
15: - [REPORT.md](REPORT.md): the detailed mathematical strategy and proposed F-role decomposition.
16: - [TEAMS.json](TEAMS.json): the twenty F groups and their five proposed research roles.
17: - [CLAIMS.md](CLAIMS.md): statements labeled PROVED_BASELINE, CONDITIONAL, OPEN_TARGET, or REFUTED, with their exact scopes.
18: - [COUNTEREXAMPLES.md](COUNTEREXAMPLES.md): mandatory falsifiers, exact scope reductions, and addressing calibration.
29: Seek a finite absolute C satisfying H(f) <= C I(f) for every Boolean sign function on every finite uniform cube, or construct a rigorously proved Boolean family with unbounded H/I. A new proper class, a refuted intermediate inequality, a failed numerical constant, and a full solution are distinct outcomes.
30: 
31: The threshold manuscript supplies structured tools: a uniform regular/LTF bound with C_reg = log2(4+e) + 2log2(e*pi) < 8.937 against character-centered influence; explicit combination and overlap bounds; corrected biased symmetric-threshold composition; a complete joint witness code; and exact selector and rare-activation identities. Regularity means a desirability order, not small weights. These results have stated hypotheses and do not settle full FEI.
32: 
33: The main supplementary opportunities are:
34: 
35: 1. Bound the complete expected cost of a normalized whole-set witness, including its roots, labels, attachments, rank terms, and normalization.
36: 2. Pay actual restriction entropy drops with one conserved physical influence budget, retaining the mismatch/KL rebate and the terminal entropy.
37: 3. Use full signed Boolean convolution identities to obtain a new valid entropy or spectral-edge inequality.
38: 4. Resolve the precise weighted correlation-lifetime target under the source's reveal policy, while keeping entropy transfer as a separate obligation.
39: 5. Improve a proper structural endpoint, such as read-k covariance, two spectral addresses, controlled selector interference, or biased weighted thresholds, and then determine what it contributes to a universal proof.
43: ## Mandatory mathematical checks
44: 
45: Read the exact statements in [COUNTEREXAMPLES.md](COUNTEREXAMPLES.md) before building on a local entropy or composition bound. In particular:
46: 
47: - Parity lifts can make aggregate erasure entropy zero while preserving arbitrary Fourier entropy and increasing influence by at most a factor two.
48: - A single physical selector can create dimension-sized erasure synergy.
51: - Coherent shared-variable products and overlapping leaf Fourier supports cannot be replaced by an incoherent mixture without a proof.
52: - Addressing requires paying its label once for a whole Fourier set; its exact values are H=2a and I=1+a/2.
53: - A normalized real spectral vector is not automatically the Fourier transform of a Boolean function.
54: 
55: All discrete Fourier entropies use base-two logarithms unless the cited theorem explicitly uses natural units. Distinguish ordinary influence, character-centered influence, dual total correlation, variance deficit, and corrected biased FEI+. Physical restriction, spectral conditioning, and evolving real wavefunctions are different operations.

5: ## Status and source conventions
6: 
7: - **PROVED_BASELINE:** a theorem/identity established in a cited primary paper or a complete proof in the recovered source. This label does not imply external peer review or proof-assistant certification of the new manuscript.
8: - **CONDITIONAL:** a proved implication whose hypothesis remains unpaid for the intended general class.
9: - **OPEN_TARGET:** an unproved mathematical goal; finite tests do not change this status.
10: - **REFUTED:** the stated assertion fails on the identified witness. The scope of the failure is part of the claim.
11: 
12: Uniform Boolean outputs are ±1, `p(S)=fhat(S)^2`, `H` is in bits, and `I=E|S|`, unless stated otherwise. Write `B=sum_i H(S_i | S_-i)` and `DTC=H-B`. Use `D_char=sum_i min(Inf_i,1-Inf_i)` for character-centered influence; it is **not** DTC. Physical restrictions fix input coordinates. Spectral conditioning and evolving real amplitudes are different operations.

FILE ~\mop_fei100\research_pushes\2026-10-11-full-fei-100-agents\COUNTEREXAMPLES.md
1: # FEI campaign counterexamples and mandatory scope tests
2: 
3: **Full FEI remains OPEN.** These examples refute specified intermediate assertions, or show that a proposed restriction retains full FEI. None is a counterexample to full FEI. Use the F01–F20 roster in `TEAMS.json`. Source aliases and log conventions are defined in `CLAIMS.md`; explicit natural-log exceptions are noted below.
4: 
5: Every proposing team must record which tests apply, why an excluded test fails its hypotheses, and whether its new conclusion is an identity, a proper-class result, a conditional reduction, or a universal claim. Historical finite checks are source-reported unless the new run actually replays them.
6: 
7: ## X001 — Parity lift hides all entropy from local erasure
8: 
9: **Owners:** F05–F07,F15,F17. **Related:** C015,C027. **Source:** ES §3.
10: 
11: For any Boolean g on n sign coordinates, let f(x,z)=g(x₁z,…,x_nz). A Fourier set S becomes S if |S| is even and S∪{z} otherwise. Therefore H(f)=H(g), every occupied set has even size, B(f)=0, and I(f)=I(g)+P(|S_g| odd)≤2I(g). Restricting the physical input z=+1 recovers arbitrary g.
12: 
13: **Defeats:** treating B=0, absence of spectral edges, or even spectral support as a certified easy leaf. The class is not closed under physical restriction. A universal bound for its entropy is full-FEI strength up to a factor two. A bound for aggregate erasure on *all restrictions* is not refuted.
14: 
15: ## X002 — One physical selector creates unbounded erasure synergy
16: 
17: **Owners:** F05–F07,F17. **Related:** C027. **Source:** ES §§1–2.
18: 
31: The exact minority influence is j_y=pδ. Both child influences are at most 5k·2^−2k→0, while B_y/j_y≥(1−δ)(k−2)→∞. In this family log₂(1+κ_min/(2j_y))=k+o(1), where κ(u)=1−max_S uhat(S)².
32: 
33: **Defeats:** B_y≤j_y[C+K·min(I(G),I(H))], and the same with max or sum, for any fixed C,K. More generally any multiplier bounded near zero child influences fails. Does not refute global non-reusing amortization that retains the logarithm or the exact mismatch/KL rebate.
34: 
35: ## X004 — Overlapping leaf supports invalidate the mixture identities
36: 
57: **Owners:** F09,F11,F14,F16. **Related:** C033. **Source:** ER §6; Shalev §1.1, https://arxiv.org/html/1806.03646v2 .
58: 
59: The real unit-norm function ψ_n(x)=n^−1/2 sum_i x_i has n Fourier atoms of mass 1/n, H=log₂n and I=1. For growing n it is not Boolean. It cannot be admitted merely because it is a normalized signed superposition of Boolean characters.
60: 
61: **Defeats:** universal entropy/influence bounds that use only normalization and allow arbitrary coherent mixtures. For a true Boolean physical splice the coefficients are (ghat±hhat)/2 on two selector-frequency sectors; neither sector may be independently substituted for a Boolean function. Full signed XOR identities must be checked, not assumed after normalization.
62: 
63: ## X007 — Shared-variable product entropy is not subadditive
64: 
69: The explicit rare-clause family also has product-entropy excess 4p²log₂(1/p)+(6/ln2−24)p²+O(p³log(1/p)), while I(f)+I(g)−I(fg)=2p². Thus no fixed multiple of this common-pivotal-overlap deficit repairs subadditivity universally.
70: 
71: **Defeats:** uncharged coherent product composition and fixed linear overlap corrections. Disjoint tensor additivity and averages over physical translations remain valid. The seven-variable table and integer histograms must be loaded from the pinned source before replay; this ledger does not replace that certificate.
72: 
73: ## X008 — Two spectral addresses already need more than constant two
74:

1: # Seed lemmas for the full-FEI campaign
2: 
3: **Status:** direct derivations, reviewed internally; separate from the completed threshold manuscript. These are not claims of novelty, external peer review, or a proof of full FEI. The hypotheses below are part of each statement. The effective-piece argument and prior mixture are useful interfaces; a dimension-independent bound on their complete expected cost remains an additional obligation.
4: 
5: **Prepared:** 11 October 2026. **Research roles concerned:** F01–F04 for reference distributions and piece costs; F15 and F17 for reductions and asymptotic diagnostics; F19 for independent reconstruction.
6: 
7: Throughout, Fourier expansions use the uniform probability measure on a finite cube. Write \(\widehat d(T)=\mathbb E[d\chi_T]\), so Parseval gives \(\|d\|_2^2=\sum_T\widehat d(T)^2\). Entropy and code lengths use \(\log_2\). The rank variable \(G\), Laplace parameter, and Euler constant \(\gamma\) use natural logarithms.
8: 
9: ## 1. Effective piece count in the multi-order rank estimate
10: 
11: ### Statement with explicit structural hypotheses
12: 
13: Let \(d_1,\ldots,d_m:\{-1,1\}^N\to\{0,\pm1\}\) be nonzero functions with pairwise disjoint physical supports. Thus \(d_l(x)d_k(x)=0\) for every \(x\) when \(l\ne k\). Put
14: 
15: \[
16: d=\sum_{l=1}^m d_l,\qquad
17: \alpha_l=\|d_l\|_2^2>0,\qquad
18: \alpha=\|d\|_2^2=\sum_l\alpha_l,
136: The multiplier argument only needs orthogonality of the pieces and the displayed piecewise Laplace estimate. Disjoint physical supports and signed fiber antichains are concrete sufficient conditions supplied by the baseline derivative partition. Orthogonality by itself does not supply the piecewise estimate.
137: 
138: Equal masses give \(K_{\mathrm{eff}}=m\). The lemma can improve a mass-concentrated derivative decomposition; it does not prove that every Boolean function admits such a decomposition, pay the remaining reference normalizer, or establish \(\sum_i I_i\ln K_{\mathrm{eff},i}=O(I)\). Any claimed FEI consequence must account for those terms separately in the complete description bound.
139: 
140: **Source pointers.** The baseline manuscript `main.tex`, SHA256 `3a395e98153f826fa9160c348e45779f204d3ea77376f40cd71ee57440ba8193`, has the signed relative-noise lemma at LaTeX label `lem:antichain-noise`, the derivative partition at `eq:pieces`, and the original multi-order proof at `lem:multi-rank`. Its archived copy is retained under `baseline/`. The direct effective-piece derivation was recorded in `supporting/joint_strategy.md`, Workstream B. The working source before campaign assembly was `final/fourier-entropy-threshold-functions/paper/main.tex`.
141:

Optimizing finite witnesses is valid because they all lower-bound the same reference mass.

364: ## 4. Review and acceptance boundaries
365: 
366: The displayed statements were checked algebraically against the baseline's signed antichain and queue interfaces. Finite computations are not used as a substitute for the proofs. The report audit also independently verified the point-defect asymptotics and the need to retain each normalization term.

The existence of a valid reference and a bound on one of its components does not settle the full entropy-influence inequality.

FILE ~\mop_fei100\research_pushes\2026-10-11-full-fei-100-agents\NATIVE_MAPPING.md

1: # Route-map addendum 11.26: critical rare-degree overpayment
2: 
3: 30 September 2026. Full FEI remains OPEN. This standalone addition leaves the canonical REPORT.md and the completed 12:48 UTC checkpoint unchanged.
4: 
5: The reviewed mathematical source is `proofnet24/P03-fei/continuations/2026-09-30-entropy-wringing/scalar-information-remainder/integrated-trajectory/critical-rare-moment/REPORT.md` at immutable commit `2af98e3a18cb84a93ec9c2a54b05f966add735b2`, on open, unmerged draft PR12. Its neighboring MANIFEST.json records the original and reviewed report hashes. Both files were read back exactly.
6: 
7: For any fixed actual indicator g maximal in raw Fourier entropy divided by energy over its physical descendants, its disjoint AND powers give genuine Boolean signs F_m with the same fixed-orientation adjusted criticality and influence tending to zero. For every fixed 0<eta<1, valid entropy-wringing addresses exist. Every such address, including any deterministic rule's admissible output, has
8: 
9:     sum_(S intersects A) |S| ln(1+|S|) Fmhat(S)^2 / I(F_m)
10:       >= q_min (1-1/m) ln(1+(m-1)J) -> infinity.
11: 
12: The exact sign law is a point mass at the empty set mixed with the tensor indicator spectrum. An irrelevant-only address fails the relative conditional-TC requirement once m ln(1/p)>ln4+rho/(1-eta). Any relevant address then yields the displayed bound by independence of the other copies and Jensen's inequality. The address entropy and conditional TC remain bounded multiples of influence, so they do not pay this full-degree moment.
13: 
14: The literal indicator is an explicit unconditional low-ratio seed. The result rules out a blanket full-degree moment substitute for the remaining rare-row entropy payment. A claim restricted to an unknown high-rho class remains conditional on the existence of an actual seed in that class. Retained-degree-only moments, direct rare-row entropy, stronger primitive/minimum-dimensional hypotheses, and full FEI are not refuted.
15: 
16: The parent and independent reviewer both read and checked the complete analytic argument. No executable replay, formal certificate, or novelty claim is made. This complements the dominant-source endpoint in ADDENDUM_11_25_DOMINANT_SOURCE_ENDPOINT.md: that positive result charges original source influence once; its rare-row full-degree remainder cannot simply be declared influence-paid.

8:

1: # Exact conditional-product endpoint: one address bit, and a two-bit boundary
2: 
3: Date: 2026-09-30. Full FEI remains OPEN. This is an exact arithmetic endpoint for the collective wringing route, not an approximate stability theorem. Publication priority is unverified.
4: 
5: ## 1. A norm-arithmetic lemma
6: 
7: Let a be a nonzero finite vector of dyadic rational amplitudes indexed by a Boolean cube. Suppose its normalized squared law is a product law. Then its squared norm w is dyadic and its reduced odd numerator is congruent to 1 modulo 4.
8: 
9: **Proof.** Remove all coordinates deterministic under the product law. On the remaining r active coordinates every amplitude is nonzero. Since ratios of nonzero amplitudes are rational, the ratio of their magnitudes along coordinate i can be written B_i/A_i, where A_i,B_i are positive coprime integers. Product magnitudes then have the form
10: 
11:     |a_s|=c product_i A_i^(1-s_i) B_i^s_i.
12: 
13: The gcd of the integer products is one: for any prime, choose in each coordinate a factor not divisible by that prime. Thus a finite integer combination of those products equals one. Since all |a_s| are dyadic, the same combination shows that c is dyadic.
14: 
15: Consequently
16: 
17:     w=c² product_i (A_i²+B_i²).
18: 
19: If exactly one of A_i,B_i is odd, the corresponding sum of squares is odd and congruent to 1 modulo 4. If both are odd, its 2-adic valuation is exactly one and its odd part is again 1 modulo 4, since odd squares are 1 modulo 8. The odd part of the square factor c² is also 1 modulo 4. Their product proves the claim. Deterministic or one-atom components are included by an empty product. QED.
20: 
21: ### Corollary 1.1
22: 
25: Indeed, write wj=uj/2^dj in reduced form. Both dj>=1. Unequal denominators give an odd numerator when brought to the larger denominator, which cannot equal its even denominator. Thus d0=d1=d. Since u0 and u1 are each 1 modulo 4, their sum is 2 modulo 4; hence 2^d has d=1, forcing u0=u1=1.
26: 
27: If a dyadic product-amplitude vector has norm squared 1/2, every nondegenerate marginal of its normalized squared law is 1/2. In the factorization above the odd part of w is one, so each primitive A_i²+B_i² is a power of two. This forces A_i=B_i=1. The number r of active fair coordinates is odd, since c²2^r=1/2 and c is dyadic. At norm squared one the same argument gives an even number of fair coordinates, recovering the prior exact-product endpoint.
28: 
29: ## 2. One address bit is paid with constant two
30: 
58:     [-4,-4,-4,4,-4,4,4,4,2,2,2,2,2,2,2,2].
59: 
60: The normalized squared law is product with marginals `(1/2,1/2,1/2,1/5)`, and its squared norm is 5/8. This is allowed by the norm-arithmetic lemma: its odd numerator is five.
61: 
62: Let h1,h2,h3 be the indicators of the three disjoint edges specified by low-three-bit values 3,5,6 respectively, with bit3 free. These edges partition the zero set of g. Introduce two address bits y1,y2 and put
63: 
71:     (5/8,1/8,1/8,1/8).
72: 
73: Every normalized conditional spectrum is product. The first has the marginals above, and the other three have `(1/2,1/2,1/2,0)`.
74: 
75: The full squared Fourier law has eight atoms of mass 1/16 and thirty-two atoms of mass 1/64. Hence H(F)=5. Directly from the four projection laws, or from pivotal-edge counting, its physical influence vector in order `(y1,y2,x0,x1,x2,x3)` is
76:

10: 
11: - A distinct seven-variable explicit Boolean f and one-point-defect multiplier g have H(fg)-H(f)-H(g)>0.1630852002095. Full integer Walsh histograms and outward rational logarithms certify it. 12: - The source-credited rare-clause family has defect 4p^2 log2(1/p)+(6/ln2-24)p^2+O(p^3 log(1/p)), while the exact influence deficit If+Ig-I(fg)=2p^2. Thus no fixed linear payment by the actual common pivotal overlap repairs product subadditivity. A logarithmic overlap cost survives only as a calibration; no universal bound of that kind is proved.
13: - Under uniform physical translations, E_z H(f g(.+z))<=Hf+Hg remains valid. The exact deficit equals H(S|S xor T)+I(U;Z), with its second term between zero and the first. The corresponding mean influence is If+Ig-2 sum_i Inf_i(f)Inf_i(g). These are retained two-input forms of the archived uncertainty mechanism, not new FEI closure.

7: ## 1. Universal target and exact accounting
8: 
9: For a genuine Boolean function on a finite uniform cube, let P(S)=fhat(S)^2, H=H_2(P), I=sum_S |S|P(S), d_i=Inf_i(f), j_i=min(d_i,1-d_i), B_i=H(S_i|S_-i), and B=sum_i B_i. Zero-mass terms are zero. Every logarithm below is base two unless written ln. D_2 denotes base-two Kullback–Leibler divergence, not order-two Rényi divergence.

13: - I(f)=d_i+[I(f_i^+)+I(f_i^-)]/2

225: 
226: The [low-influence source note](https://github.com/yspennstate/math-open-problems/blob/ecc6da6b0c2514fd885bd2c4de03eb6f26f13f3b/research_pushes/2026-09-30-fei-log-allocation/LOW_INFLUENCE_SOURCE_NOTE.md) credits Shalev, arXiv:1806.03646v2, Theorem 3 and Section 3.2: a universal FEI theorem on `I(f)<=2^(-s(n))`, `s(n)=o(n)`, already implies full FEI; the activation construction is attributed there to Wan–Wright–Wu. 242: **Retain the history refund.** [The exact history note](https://github.com/yspennstate/math-open-problems/blob/c63180b23321ce9130dbcbab825546f6c14923d0/proofnet24/P03-fei/continuations/2026-09-30-entropy-wringing/scalar-information-remainder/integrated-trajectory/BOOLEAN_HISTORY_REFUND.md) expresses C as the pivotal Jensen envelope minus conditional information in the actual record. On AND_n the envelope grows like nI while the true cost is O(I). The prephysical record refund integrates to TC(P)+an error in[0,I], but the full refund also contains actual K minus the no-history physical information Kbar. Kbar itself can be order nI and cannot be dropped. The AND leading constants and their integrable domination are recorded; this is an overcharge guard, not a new FEI bound.

244: **Stronger source structure, not independent sphere rows.** In [the biased-Boolean structure note](https://github.com/yspennstate/math-open-problems/blob/c63180b23321ce9130dbcbab825546f6c14923d0/proofnet24/P03-fei/continuations/2026-09-30-entropy-wringing/scalar-information-remainder/integrated-trajectory/BIASED_BOOLEAN_STRUCTURE.md), the fixed-time clicked set A is sufficient; detailed click times are ancillary given A. With theta=1−exp(−t), its unnormalized amplitude b_A(x) is exactly the biased Fourier coefficient of the actual Boolean function eta↦f(x eta), where E eta_i=sqrt(1−theta). Thus mu_theta^x(A)=b_A(x)² obeys the complete weighted biased product identities, and all x-rows are translates of one f. The exact C functional uses adjacent coefficients, or equivalently the biased transforms of actual complementary ternary pairs.
245: 
246: A proved sufficient reduction retains this structure:
247: 
249:     Q_bias=integral_0^1 E_x[sum_i H_mu_theta^x(A_i|A_-i)] dtheta/theta.

1: # B09 — product-biased restriction uncertainty: a prime-gate counterexample and an endpoint-safe repair
2: 
3: **Run:** `20261011T195500Z-account01-B09-biased-restriction-uncertainty`  
4: **Date:** 2026-10-11  
5: **Classification:** `PROVED_AUXILIARY_LEMMA_AND_REFUTED_HELPER`  
6: **Full uniform-cube FEI:** **OPEN / NOT PROVED.**
7: 
8: ## 0. Result in one line
9: 
10: For a genuine Boolean gate under an arbitrary product measure, the physical-block entropy decrement still splits exactly as
11: 
12: \[
13: L_A=M_A+J_A,
14: \]
15: 
16: where `M_A` is the actual conditional spectral-label entropy and `J_A` is the physical-child mixture information.  The uniform estimate `J_A<=M_A` is **false** at nonuniform biases, already for the two-bit AND sign gate.  The following endpoint-safe replacement is universal:
17: 
18: \[
60: - account02/B13 physical block/Holevo identity, blob `69c9c84b24b7a201d46f6071591fafcc874aea75`;
61: - current B05 rare-restriction report, blob `1b7e3cb572354cac4e506d7de0a3ed8af720472b`;
62: - the corrected Numaro biased-prime falsifications and composition-prime reduction reports found in the September archive.
63: 
64: Primary literature checked:
65: 
66: - O'Donnell--Tan, *A composition theorem for the Fourier Entropy-Influence conjecture*, arXiv:1304.1347.  It is the source for the product-biased disjoint composition framework; no novelty is claimed for that framework.
67: - Maassen--Uffink, *Generalized entropic uncertainty relations*, Phys. Rev. Lett. 60 (1988), 1103--1106, DOI `10.1103/PhysRevLett.60.1103`.
68: 
69: The new point of this run is the exact specialization of the non-mutually-unbiased transform to a physical product-biased restriction, its explicit endpoint tax, and the source-valid prime-gate falsifier to the tax-free version.  Publication novelty has not been independently reviewed.
128: \]
129: 
130: All signs, child normalizations, physical branch probabilities, and zero masses remain in this identity.
131: 
132: ## 3. PROVED: biased uncertainty bound with explicit rare-event tax
133: 
138: ### Proof
139: 
140: Fix `T subseteq B` with `q_T>0` and define the normalized spectral-fiber vector
141: 
142: \[
143: a_R=\frac{\widehat f_p(R\cup T)}{\sqrt{q_T}},
145: \]
146: 
147: Its squared entries have entropy `H(S_A | S_B=T)`.  Define the normalized physical posterior vector
148: 
149: \[
150: b_z=\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{p_A}(z)}\,\widehat{f_z}_{p_B}(T)}{\sqrt{q_T}},
275: Thus the tax-free extension fails by more than `0.1195` bits on the smallest nonlinear genuine Boolean gate.  This is a gate-helper counterexample, not an FEI counterexample: it says nothing about unbounded `H/I`.
276: 
277: ## 5. Composition and prime-gate consequences
278: 
279: O'Donnell--Tan's product-biased composition theorem already reduces disjoint read-once compositions to gate inequalities at the induced output biases.  The exact matrix in (3.1) is compatible with that reduction: it is a tensor product on disjoint blocks, and both `Delta_A` and the aligned `M_A,J_A` ledgers add.
280: 
281: Therefore:
282: 
283: 1. decomposable gates do not create a new violation of (0.1) beyond their factors;
284: 2. the two-bit AND gate proves that a prime-gate proof cannot simply transplant the uniform `J<=M` statement to induced nonuniform biases;
285: 3. a composition proof using this uncertainty route must either pay `Delta_A(p)` once, amortize it against a hierarchy credit, or exploit stronger Boolean phase constraints;
286: 4. because `Delta(p)=p log_2(1/p)+O(p^2)`, replacing it by `O(p)` is impossible at the endpoint scale relevant to rare activation.
287: 
288: The unpaid universal implication remains a dimension-free payment of the actual conditional label cost `M_A` plus the nonreused endpoint tax by the appropriate biased influence/gate potential.
298: \]
299: 
300: Thus the uniform inequality is sharp and the whole address label is shared, rather than charged separately for each spectral coordinate.  Deterministic checks cover `m=1,2,3`.
301: 
302: ### Sparse defects and rare restrictions
303: 
308: For exact disjoint products and aligned blocks, the spectral law, physical posterior channel, `M`, `J`, and `Delta` all tensorize additively.  The checker includes quadratic bent tensors through seven factors.  This theorem makes no continuity claim for coherent near-tensor surgery; such a claim would need to pay the known `alpha^2 log(1/alpha^2)` term.
309: 
310: ### Actual composition-prime gate
311: 
312: The exact source-reconstructed 18-bit Numaro gate was checked at the two previously adversarial biases `p_0=3/8` and `p_0=223/456`, with all other inputs uniform, plus the uniform point.
313: 
356: **New proved movement:** the exact restriction identity extends to arbitrary product biases, but the conjugate bases cease to be mutually unbiased.  The loss is universally bounded by the explicit additive tax `Delta_A(p)`.  The tax-free extension is rigorously refuted on the two-bit AND gate by (4.4).
357: 
358: **New first missing implication (OPEN):** construct a nonreusing composition/restriction potential that pays
359: 
360: \[
361: M_A+\Delta_A(p)
362: \]
363: 
364: by biased influence uniformly over composition-prime gates and arbitrary interior biases, or improve the Maassen--Uffink tax using a specifically Boolean constraint while retaining the exact two-bit counterexample and the rare endpoint asymptotic.
365: 
366: **Concrete next action:** solve the one-bit block optimization under Boolean-realizable coefficient fibers, beginning with all essential two-input gates.  Determine the sharp scalar replacement for `Delta(p)` and test whether its endpoint coefficient can be transported through the O'Donnell--Tan gate cocycle without being paid repeatedly.

1:      ENTROPY AND SINGULARITY OF SPECTRAL AND PIVOTAL SETS IN
2:                                              PLANAR PERCOLATION
3: 
4:                                                                YITZCHAK SHMALO
5: 
6:             Abstract. The spectral sample and pivotal set of a percolation crossing have the same one- and
7:             two-coordinate inclusion probabilities under the uniform product measure. We prove that both
8:             Shannon entropies are comparable to total influence for critical square-lattice bond and triangular-
9:             lattice site crossings, with matching estimates at every retained nonroot spatial resolution. The
10:             triangular entropy bounds are uniform over a class of inhomogeneous near-critical product measures.
11:             For critical triangular-site square crossings, the two laws are asymptotically mutually singular: an
12:             elementary triangular face is forbidden in the pivotal set and occurs with positive density in a
13:             large spectral sample. Every fixed face density below an explicit threshold has nonempty spectral
14:             probability comparable to R24(R)2, while the nonempty overlap of the two laws lies between
15:             R-11/12+o(1) and R-1/2+o(1), and equals R-11/12+o(1) if the Garban­Pete­Schramm bound on small
16:             spectral samples extends to pivotal sets. Their minimum expected symmetric-difference distance
17:             over all couplings is comparable to total influence. Geometric separation persists after independent
18:             thinning whenever the retention probability R satisfies R3 R24(R)  ; the face detector fails

1:      FOURIER ENTROPY OF THRESHOLD FUNCTIONS AND THEIR
2:                                               COMPOSITIONS
3: 
4:                                                         YITZCHAK SHMALO
5: 
6:      Abstract. We prove the Fourier entropy­influence inequality for every linear threshold function,
7:      without restrictions on its weights or threshold. The argument applies to regular Boolean functions
8:      and gives an O(log(m + 1)) bound for arbitrary Boolean combinations of m such functions. These
9:      estimates hold after multiplication by any coordinate character and imply bounds by influence
10:      minus variance. We also obtain an O(log(k + 1)) bound for unions of regular functions in which
11:      each variable occurs at most k times. For symmetric thresholds under arbitrary product measures,
12:      we prove the strengthened entropy inequality needed for composition. Consequently, read-once
13:      formulas of arbitrary depth and fan-in over symmetric threshold and parity gates satisfy FEI. The
14:      proofs use antichain estimates for pivotal fibers, spectral reference measures, and product-measure
15:      composition.

1:      FOURIER ENTROPY OF THRESHOLD FUNCTIONS AND THEIR
2:                                               COMPOSITIONS
3: 
4:                                                         YITZCHAK SHMALO
5: 
6:      Abstract. We prove the Fourier entropy­influence inequality for every linear threshold function,
7:      with a constant below 8.937 and no restrictions on its weights or threshold. The argument applies
8:      to regular Boolean functions and gives logarithmic bounds for their Boolean combinations and for
9:      unions with bounded variable overlap. Character multiplication preserves the estimates and yields
10:      bounds by influence minus variance. Under arbitrary product measures, symmetric thresholds satisfy
11:      the strengthened inequality needed for read-once composition, uniformly in depth and fan-in. A
12:      joint description of each Fourier set refines the overlap estimate and recovers constant bounds for
13:      addressing. Exact selector identities extend the bound to orthogonal multiplexers with shared data.
14:      Rare activation preserves the optimal constant of unrestricted FEI. The proofs combine pivotal
15:      antichains, spectral reference measures, and product-measure composition.
16: 
17:                                        1. Introduction
18:

1: # exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

3: A023 / B05, run 20261011T221608Z-account05-A023-amortized-rarity-budget. All cubes are uniform, all functions are Boolean signs, and entropy/KL/logarithms are in bits. Set c=1/ln(2). For any Boolean core g, r>=1 fresh controls, p=2^-r, and a=1-Eg in [0,2], define F=1+A_r(g-1), where A_r specifies one physical control point. The stopping tree queries these controls in a fixed order, stops that component on the first mismatch, and otherwise stops at g after r matches. It is physical, adaptive through this stopping rule, deterministic, and finite.
4: 
5: Write J_v=mu_v[log2(1/mu_v)-D2(nu_v||Q_v)], with mu_v the MAGNITUDE mismatch (minority fiber) mass, Q_v the average child squared Fourier law, and nu_v its normalized minority law. Zero mismatch means zero cost. The first target is the complete bound
6: 
7:     sum_v w_v J_v <= K rpa,   K=1+c+8c/e < 7,
8:     sum_v w_v (J_v+3c mu_v) + pH(g) <= 12rpa+pH(g).

Widths r, bent dimensions k, chosen vertices, and number of components are all independent and unbounded. For this family control-first J+terminal<=7I, the full sufficient mismatch ledger including 3c mu is <=12I, and H<=8I. Selector-first J+terminal<=8I and its full sufficient ledger is <=13I. This includes the original B05 quadratic witness with r=2k. No assertion of universal tree selection or full FEI is made.

28: ## 1. Node definitions and the complete entropy allowance
29: 
30: For child coefficients x_T,y_T, let Q_T=(x_T^2+y_T^2)/2, e_T=(x_T-y_T)^2/4 and m_T=(|x_T|-|y_T|)^2/4=min(e_T,Q_T-e_T). Put d=sum e_T, mu=sum m_T<=d, L=sum Q_T h2(e_T/Q_T). The exact decrement is Delta H=L+JS(x^2,y^2). The signed logarithmic allocation is J^sgn=sum e_T log2(Q_T/e_T); the required minority allocation is J=sum m_T log2(Q_T/m_T). Each is its own normalized KL expression; they must not be interchanged at a sign-changing endpoint.
31: 
32: Elementary entropy expansion gives J<=L and L<=J^sgn+c d. The first follows because the other half of binary entropy is nonnegative; the second follows from -(1-t)ln(1-t)<=t. Separately L<=J+c mu. The Hellinger inequality JS<=2c mu gives Delta H<=J+3c mu. For completeness: write two probabilities as s(1+t),s(1-t). The nats JS contribution is s[(1+t)ln(1+t)+(1-t)ln(1-t)]/2, at most s(1-sqrt(1-t^2)). The difference starts at zero and has derivative t/sqrt(1-t^2)-artanh(t)>=0, since this derivative's derivative is nonnegative. Summing gives JS_nat<=sum (|x_T|-|y_T|)^2/2=2mu. Thus no unreviewed restriction theorem is needed for this allowance.
33: 
34: ## 2. Arbitrary-bias control tree and its retained KL payment
35: 
36: At the unique active node with s remaining controls set u=2^-s and d_s=u a. Its children are 1 and 1+A_(s-1)(g-1). The physical reach probability is w_s=p/u. The empty packet has Q0=1-2d_s+2d_s^2 and signed mismatch e0=d_s^2. In every other packet one child coefficient is zero, so e_T=m_T=Q_T/2, and the total such mismatch is d_s-d_s^2. Hence, for all s including the last one,
37: 
38:     J_s^sgn=d_s-d_s^2+d_s^2 log2(Q0/d_s^2).
39: 
40: If s>=2, the active child's mean 1-2ua is nonnegative; consequently magnitude mismatch agrees with signed mismatch. For s=1 and Eg<0 the empty minority is (1-a/2)^2 rather than a^2/4. We retain the genuine minority cost and only use J_s<=L_s<=J_s^sgn+c d_s. This handles g=-1, g=+1, zero coefficients, and the last-layer sign change. For a=0 the whole tree is constant and all costs are zero.

61: ## 3. First-revelation ledger and no reuse across descendants
62: 
63: Give this control block one identifier when its first control is queried. Its account holds exactly B_ctrl=rpa, consisting of the distinct physical control-coordinate influences. Its single bill is the complete expected sum of the J_s charges; descendants add subcharges to that bill and cannot open a second account for the same control event. It is closed when the block first becomes known inactive (first mismatch) or known active (r matches). The core account is pI(g), belongs only to the terminal core, and pays pH(g). The two accounts are disjoint; neither is pledged to both a parent and a descendant. This is a first-revelation payment in the sense of the first physical revelation process of a declared event, not an oracle revelation of an unqueried event.
64: 
65: KL credit is generated at the node where its actual (nu_s,Q_s) pair is defined, and consumed once in that node's J_s=mu_s log(1/mu_s)-mu_s D2(nu_s||Q_s). We neither transfer a KL value to another reference law nor claim that the same event earns a fresh credit at every descendant. The paid quantity is the completed block bill, with all these distinct conditional subbills included exactly once. The logarithm is not discarded: the formula in section 2 bounds the post-KL bill explicitly.
66: 
67: On the positive-mean core subfamily (a<=1), mu_s=d_s and the unrebated weighted rarity sum is exactly
68: 
69:     p a [r(r+1)/2+r log2(1/a)].
70: 
83:     H(F)<=pH(g)+[4+2c(1+1/e)]rpa < pH(g)+8rpa.
84: 
85: The strict numerical constant follows from c<81/56 and e>65/24: 2c(1+1/e)<7209/1820<4. This improves the conservative mismatch-ledger constant but does not replace the requested J_tree certificate. If H(g)<=C0 I(g), then H(F)<=max(C0,8)I(F); the full sufficient mismatch ledger is <=max(C0,12)I(F). Arbitrary cores still require an actual terminal certificate.

87: ## 5. A complete unbounded family: activated arbitrary bent point-surgery selectors
88: 
103: ## 6. Selector-first complete tree and comparison
104: 
105: Query t at the root, then query controls in each child, stopping on their first mismatch, with terminals b or b'. Let a_*=(1-Eb+1-Eb')/2=a and Ibar=(I(b)+I(b'))/2. Root disagreement is d=pdelta. Because the root children differ at exactly one point of the (r+k)-cube, every Fourier ratio is an even integer: an integer Walsh sum of 2^(r+k) signs is even. Thus the signed mismatch e=d^2 is the minority in every packet, mu=d. Its exact bill is sum_T e log(Q/e), and its KL representation implies
106: 
107:     J_root<=d log2(1/d)=pdelta(r+k).
108: 
122: At a node querying component j, child Fourier coefficients are x_T b_R,y_T b_R. Their Q and m are multiplied by b_R^2; the ratio Q/m is unchanged, and sum_R b_R^2=1. Thus J, mu and queried influence are precisely the component's quantities, even with earlier terminal cores and later unresolved components present. Averaging over earlier histories restores the original reach weights for component j. Hence total J and expected terminal entropy are the sums of the component bills, and all bounds in sections 5-6 add. The simultaneous rarity events never share an account or a physical coordinate. This proves the stated unbounded-dimensional, unbounded-component family, rather than merely finitely checking a quadratic example.
123: 
124: First unsupported universal line: every arbitrary Boolean f admits such a controlled-event decomposition, or another physical stopping rule with the same complete linear bill and uniformly certified terminal cores. Neither is proved. Allowing arbitrary g in section 2 without its terminal certificate leaves pH(g) unpaid; the threshold paper's Corollary 8.10 explains why this cannot be treated as a negligible remainder.

FILE ~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\agents\A041\runs\20261011T214208Z-account05-A041-weighted-threshold-fei\REPORT.md
1: # A041: biased weighted pivotal intervals and a necessary endpoint tax
2: 
3: ## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units
4: 
5: The packet target is an arity-independent replacement probability or entropy estimate for actual Boolean weighted thresholds under arbitrary product biases, surviving the three recovered physical-input TC/pivotal normalization controls. The stronger campaign target remains corrected FEI+: `H_mu^+(f) <= C [I_mu(f)-Var_mu(f)]` for all real weights, all arities, and all product biases. **That stronger target and full uniform FEI remain OPEN.**
6: 
7: Two source-complete auxiliary results are proved here, pending independent review:
8: 
9: 1. For the i-th pivotal interval and every integer r>=1, coordinates of weight at least w_i/r have fiber height at most r. With `L=2 sum min(q_j,1-q_j)` on those coordinates and derivative spectral rank K, `E_nu (1-epsilon)^K <= min(1,r/sqrt(epsilon L))`. The positive logarithmic rank score `ln^+[L/(r^2(K+1))]` has mean at most `3+ln 2`. A simultaneous choice among all dyadic scales, using prior `pi_k=1/[(k+1)(k+2)]` and score `sup_k pi_k^2 L_k/[4^k(K_k+1)]`, retains that same constant. Summing the score against the actual coordinate Fourier masses costs at most `(3+ln 2) I` in **nats**. The proof covers arbitrary positive real weights; orientation covers signed weights, and contraction covers zero weights/deterministic biases.
10: 2. For actual Boolean `f=1-2XY`, `X~Ber(p)`, `Y~Ber(1/4)`, revealing X, the difference between physical-child mixture information J and conditional spectral-label entropy M satisfies `lim_{p->0}(J-M)/[p log_2(1/p)]=1/2`. Thus a scalar tax valid for every Boolean one-bit reveal must have asymptotic lower coefficient at least 1/2; no finite `Kp` tax works. This result is in **bits**.

The old available upper tax has coefficient one; its optimal coefficient is not determined. The lower-bound family establishes necessity through an actual signed difference.

28: 
29: Use x ln(1/x)=0 at x=0. Formula (1) extends continuously to the closed bias cube. Any S intersecting a deterministic coordinate has w_S=0, while coefficients on the remaining coordinates are those of the actual restricted Boolean table. This proves path-independent contraction and transfers any fixed-arity inequality E<=C_nat D on the entire interior to its boundary. It does not bound the ratio at a boundary point with D=0 merely by continuity.
30: 
31: More precisely, along a fixed real-analytic one-parameter path into an endpoint, write a nonzero atom as w_S(t)=W_S t^r(1+O(t)), c_S(t)=b_S t^s(1+O(t)), W_S>0, b_S nonzero, r,s nonnegative integers. Then
32: 
35:          +O(t^(r+2s+1)(1+ln(1/t))).                    (2)
36: 
37: The same P(S) multiplied by |S|-1 supplies its deficit term. When r=0 the O(t) formulation still follows from analyticity. Identically zero atoms contribute zero. For c_S identically of magnitude one, E_S is identically zero. All logarithms in (2) arise from a vanishing normalized c_S, not from the boundary factor w_S. Summing this finite list gives an algorithm for symbolic leading orders along any such path. Formula (2) is fixed-table/path asymptotics, not an arity-uniform remainder or an assertion for nonanalytic paths. Arbitrary paths still have the continuity statement (1).

85: 
86: For a unit spectral atom P(U)=1 on the contracted interior cube, Parseval implies f=+/-phi_U pointwise. Since |f|=1 and the coordinates have full independent support, varying any one coordinate in U shows that its two phi_i magnitudes must agree; this forces p_i=1/2. Hence f is a signed parity on fair coordinates U (irrelevant coordinates may have any bias). E=0 and D=|U|-1 for U nonempty. U empty gives a constant and D=0. In particular a unit singleton atom is a fair dictator; a higher-degree unit atom is not a zero-deficit endpoint. Its neighboring slice obeys (4)--(6) with D0=|U|-1. At a deterministic endpoint the assertion is made only after contraction; the unreduced basis is undefined there.

FILE ~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\agents\A056\runs\20261011T214407Z-account05-A056-balanced-composition\REPORT.md
1: # A056: balanced disjoint substitution and its physical restrictions
2: 
3: ## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units
4: 
5: For every finite k and pairwise disjoint physical coordinate blocks B_i, let g_i:{-1,1}^{B_i}->{-1,1}, let h:{-1,1}^k->{-1,1}, and put F=h(g_1,...,g_k). Physical coordinates are independent uniform signs. Entropy H is Shannon entropy in bits of the **entire** squared Fourier spectrum, including the empty coefficient; I is expected Fourier-set size, equivalently total flip influence. No restriction on the mean of h is needed. First assume every g_i is balanced: E g_i=0. Then
6: 
7:     E F = E h,
8:     Var F = Var h,
9:     H(F) = H(h) + sum_i Inf_i(h) H(g_i),
10:     I(F) = sum_i Inf_i(h) I(g_i),
11:     Inf_x(F) = Inf_i(h) Inf_x(g_i),  x in B_i.
12: 
13: This packet proves these identities, extends them to arbitrary child means, and identifies precisely what must be updated after a physical restriction. These are composition baseline identities, not a proof of unrestricted FEI. The complete analytic proof is local; independent acceptance is pending.

33: ### Arbitrary means: reexpanded outer gate
34: 
35: Let m_i=E g_i and v_i=1-m_i^2. First absorb all deterministic children (v_i=0) into h by fixing those output coordinates to m_i. This is exact because a Boolean uniform-cube function of mean +/-1 is constant at every input. Reindex the remaining children; now v_i>0. Their independent output law is mu, with P(Y_i=+1)=(1+m_i)/2. In its orthonormal basis psi_i(y)=(y_i-m_i)/sqrt(v_i), write
36: 
37:     h(y)=sum_T a_mu(T) product_{i in T} psi_i(y).
38: 
58:     Inf_x(F)=(w_i/v_i)Inf_x(g_i), x in B_i.                        (5)
59: 
60: Equations (2)-(5) are valid at every finite dimension, for arbitrary nonconstant child means. All entropy terms are finite; no limiting assertion is needed. If every child was deterministic, F is constant and all entropies and influences are zero. Outer irrelevant coordinates have w_i=0 and require no child contribution. Balanced children give v_i=1, h2(v_i)=0 and the uniform outer law, recovering the first formulas.
61: 
62: For comparison with Proposition 7.1, the strong-entropy convention must be distinguished from conditional nonempty entropy. The convention that actually composes is Eplus(g)=sum_{U nonempty} c(U)^2 log2(1/c(U)^2) on uniform physical inputs, and Eplus_mu(h)=sum_{T nonempty} P_mu(T)log2(product v_i/P_mu(T)) on outputs. Then exactly
63: 
70: ### Physical restrictions: what survives, and what changes
71: 
72: Fix actual physical coordinates W to a deterministic assignment rho. In each block replace g_i by g_i^rho=g_i restricted to W intersect B_i. Remaining inputs retain the independent uniform law and disjoint supports. Therefore (2)-(5) hold with the **new** means m_i^rho, variances v_i^rho, absorbed deterministic children, and the Fourier expansion of the reduced outer gate under the **new** output product law. They do not generally hold with old a_mu or old w_i. The balanced formulas are available at a reached branch when every remaining nonconstant child is balanced after absorption of constants. If only one child is restricted, all untouched initially balanced children remain balanced, but the changed child must be checked. Balance of that child is sufficient; it is not claimed logically necessary for accidental numerical equality in a special gate.
73: 
74: For a single initially balanced child j, write the uniform outer polynomial as h=A(y_-j)+y_j B(y_-j). Let a_T=Ahat(T), b_T=Bhat(T), and let the changed child's mean be m, variance v=1-m^2>0. Other children stay balanced. Recenter:

82: ### Exact witness against retaining the old balanced formulas
83: 
84: Let h(y,z)=AND_2(y,z) in sign notation: +1 only when both inputs are +1. Its uniform spectrum has four coefficients of magnitude 1/2, so E h=-1/2, H(h)=2, and Inf_y(h)=Inf_z(h)=1/2. Let the first initially balanced child be majority on three signs (x,a,b), and the second be a fresh dictator z. Physically fixing x=+1 changes the first child to OR_2(a,b), with mean 1/2, variance 3/4, H=2 and I=1. The restricted F=AND_2(OR_2(a,b),z) has constant coefficient -1/4, z coefficient 3/4, and the other six coefficients of magnitude 1/4. Its spectrum is seven atoms 1/16 and one atom 9/16. Hence
85: 
86:     E F=-1/4,     I(F)=5/4,
87:     H(F)=4-(9/16)log2(9)=4-(9/8)log2(3).
91: ### Scope of consequences
92: 
93: Ordinary bounds H(h)<=C I(h) and H(g_i)<=C I(g_i) alone do not give the same C under the balanced identity: the outer H(h) remains an additional term. A sufficient balanced compositional invariant is H(q)<=C[I(q)-Var(q)] for the gate and all children. Since Var(g_i)=1, the formulas give H(F)<=C[I(F)-Var(F)]. This is a conditional consequence of explicitly stated gate/child bounds; those bounds are not proved for arbitrary Boolean functions here. After a physical restriction, either verify balance again or use product-biased identities and gate hypotheses at the new means. An arbitrary weighted threshold theorem established only for uniform inputs cannot be applied at newly biased child outputs. No overlap theorem, selector credit, or full FEI conclusion follows from these identities alone.

The unpaid next research term is a quantitative entropy/influence payment for changed biased gate laws or overlapping-block coherent coefficients; it is not supplied by disjoint identities.

This is finite corroboration of the analytic proof, not an all-dimensional inference.

FILE ~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\agents\A096\runs\20261011T215017Z-account05-A096-live-gap-ledger\REPORT.md
1: # A096: source-bound gap ledger and sharp fixed-pair rare-bias coefficient
2: 
3: ## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units
4: 
5: Packet A096, B20. Full uniform-cube FEI remains OPEN. The nearest unpaid lemma selected is the sharp one-bit rare-bias tax in B09's section 8, with A046's AND lower-bound work treated as existing work rather than repeated. The final result is the dimension-uniform upper tax J_X-M_X<=h2(r)-h2(1-sqrt(1-r)), r=min(p,1-p), and the sharp universal rare-endpoint coefficient 1/2. Two complete local claims await independent review; no third candidate is opened.
6: 
7: Fix a finite residual cube with any fixed interior product measure, and two actual Boolean sign children G,H. A new independent selector X has Pr(X=1)=p, and F_p equals G at X=1 and H at X=0. All entropies are in bits and residual parameters and children are fixed as p tends to zero. Write a_T=hat G(T), b_T=hat H(T), c=E[GH], D={T:b_T !=0}, A=sum_{T in D}a_T^2. Let M_p=H(S_X|S_residual), and J_p=I(X;T) in the actual physical-child/spectral experiment. Then
8: 
9:     J_p-M_p = kappa(G,H) p log2(1/p) + O_{G,H,measure}(p),
10:     kappa = 2(c-A),
11:     -4 <= kappa <= 1/2,
12:     1/2-kappa = 2 ||P_D a-c b||_2^2 + 2(c-1/2)^2.
13: 
14: Consequently the supremum, over fixed actual finite Boolean child pairs, of the limiting normalized tax is exactly 1/2. Equality holds exactly when c=1/2 and P_D a=b/2. The example H=1 and G=(-1)^{y1 y2} on two uniform residual bits has c=a_empty=1/2 and realizes equality. Multiplying both children by any residual character gives nonconstant common-child examples as well. The claim is a fixed-pair endpoint theorem, not a dimension-uniform remainder bound or a full FEI theorem.

23: ## Disjoint coefficient reconstruction, all child biases
24: 
25: Let independent product physical blocks B_i carry Boolean f_i with mean m_i and variance v_i=1-m_i^2>0. Their sign outputs have probabilities q_i=(1+m_i)/2 and are independent. Expand outer Boolean g under this induced product law nu in psi_i(z)=(z_i-m_i)/sqrt(v_i). The normalized centered child has expansion
26: 
27:     (f_i-m_i)/sqrt(v_i)=sum_{T nonempty in B_i} fhat_i(T) phi_T/sqrt(v_i).
28: 
32:     Fhat_mu(empty)=ghat_nu(empty),       F=g(f_1,...,f_k).
33: 
34: There is one decomposition, so no coherent cross term is discarded. Sample A from ghat_nu^2; for each i in A sample a nonempty T_i from fhat_i^2/v_i; output their union. This experiment is bijective on its positive outcomes. Set w_i=I_i^nu(g), a_i=w_i/v_i. Then
35: 
36:     V_mu(F)=V_nu(g),
37:     I_mu(F)=sum_i a_i I_mu_i(f_i),
44: For balanced children v_i=1 and nu is uniform, hence H(F)=H(g)+sum_i Inf_i(g)H(f_i). Ordinary FEI estimates H<=CI alone do not cancel the outer entropy. Strong deficit estimates are sufficient. For unbalanced children, retaining the old uniform outer spectrum is unsupported.
45: 
46: Boundary cases are exact. If v_i=0, freeze that child output and delete its gate input BEFORE division; at product-bias endpoints delete deterministic physical inputs first. Functions differing only on zero-probability assignments are identified after that restriction. If all children are constant, F is constant and H=I=delta=0. If outer input i is irrelevant, w_i=0 and its child makes no contribution. For interior nonconstant children a_i=E[(D_i g)^2], where D_i is the sign derivative on other outputs, so 0<=a_i<=1: a tiny v_i introduces no intrinsic blowup of this physical multiplier. A biased literal has Hplus=delta=0 although ordinary spectral H=h2(v_i) has a divergent H/I endpoint ratio. A uniform balanced Boolean of I=1 is a signed dictator (spectral support only on singletons and Booleanity forces a single coefficient), so its entropy is zero; division by I-1 at this terminal is forbidden.
47: 
48: At any physical restriction cylinder, unrevealed inputs are still independent product inputs and blocks remain disjoint. Recompute m_i, v_i, the reduced gate and its induced nu. For one changed child, write g=A+z_i B before restriction. If its new mean is m, the new outer polynomial is A+mB+sqrt(1-m^2)psi_i B; coefficients not containing i are Ahat(T)+m Bhat(T), whose squared values contain 2m Ahat(T)Bhat(T). At m=+/-1 freeze the gate input. Conditioning on an OUTPUT event is not a physical cylinder and does not justify the same product premise. This reconstructs A056's principal claims without borrowing its implementation.
49: 
52: The exact identity admits symmetric threshold gates under every induced product bias with C_T=80+460/ln2, and parity with 2/ln2, using the cited gate lemmas. Uniform regular gates have a strong bound 4C_reg; an arbitrary weighted LTF internal gate is admissible by that uniform theorem only when all remaining nonconstant children are balanced. Uniform-input LTF terminal blocks can be unbalanced outputs: their bound Hplus<=4C_reg delta is a physical-input statement, and the next gate must then accept those biases. Negations preserve corrected entropy/deficit when the corresponding input law is flipped.
53:

The identities neither establish that prime estimate nor justify overlap of physical child blocks. The dyadic gate-potential formulation in biased.tex is FEI-equivalent, not a solution: finite uniform encoders realize only dyadic q; extension of the infimum-defined potential to nondyadic q is an additional unproved step. FILE ~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\source_material\reductions\paper\biased.tex
1: \section{Product measures and obstructions to threshold normalization}
2: \label{biasfull:section}
3: 
4: The generalized FEI inequality of O'Donnell and Tan~\cite{ODT13}
5: uses a corrected spectral entropy under product measures. Symmetric
6: threshold gates satisfy this inequality under arbitrary product inputs
7: in~\cite{Threshold}. The normalization estimates used for those gates
8: cannot be extended to weighted thresholds without a different argument.
9: We give explicit counterexamples to both a conditional-total-correlation
10: estimate and its proposed pivotal-credit replacement. Their genuine
11: corrected entropy-to-deficit ratios tend to \(2\).
12: 
13: \subsection{Normalization and exact composition}
14: 
15: Let \(Y_i\in\{0,1\}\) be independent with
16: \(\P(Y_i=1)=q_i\in(0,1)\), and let
17: \[
18:  \phi_i(Y_i)=\frac{Y_i-q_i}{\sqrt{q_i(1-q_i)}},
27: \]
28: Its corrected entropy is
29: \begin{equation}\label{biasfull:hplus}
30:  \HH_\mu^+(f)=\sum_{S\ne\varnothing}P_f^\mu(S)
31:                       \log\frac{v_S}{P_f^\mu(S)}.
32: \end{equation}
48: \(\HH^+(f)=\HH(f)-h_0(f)\).
49: 
50: We use the following exact form of disjoint composition.
51: 
52: \begin{lemma}[Product-measure composition]\label{biasfull:composition}
53: Let \(g_i\) be Boolean functions on disjoint variable blocks with product
54: input laws \(\mu_i\). Let \(p_i=\P(g_i=1)\), \(V_i=4p_i(1-p_i)>0\), and
55: let \(\nu\) be the product law with these output probabilities.
76: The squared coefficient is therefore the outer spectral mass times the
77: conditional nonempty spectral masses of the occupied children.
78: Taking the logarithm in~\eqref{biasfull:hplus} proves
79: \eqref{biasfull:comph}; summing degrees proves~\eqref{biasfull:compi}.
80: Finally \(V_\mu(F)=V_\nu(G)\), so subtraction proves
81: \eqref{biasfull:compdelta}.
250: \end{proof}
251: 
252: \begin{theorem}[Failure of weighted-threshold normalization estimates]
253: \label{biasfull:weightedcounter}
254: For every \(m\ge2\), specialize Lemma~\ref{biasfull:mixture} to
255: \[
367: \((2+4/\ln2)\,4xy(1-x)(1-y)\).
368: Thus \(\HH_o^+=O(ma^2)\).
369: The heavy child is a dictator and has zero corrected entropy and
370: zero deficit. The light-block multiplier in
371: Lemma~\ref{biasfull:composition} is \(1-q_0\), so
372: \[
373:  \Delta_\mu(f)=\Delta_o+(1-q_0)\Delta_\mu(g)\sim4ma,\qquad
374:  \HH_\mu^+(f)=\HH_o^++(1-q_0)\HH_\mu^+(g)\sim8ma.
426: Let \(V_i=4p_i(1-p_i)\).
427: If ordinary uniform FEI is assumed with a constant \(C\), then
428: Lemma~\ref{biasfull:composition} applied to \(F=G(g_1,\ldots,g_k)\)
429: gives exactly
430: \begin{equation}\label{biasfull:encoding}
431:  \HH_\nu^+(G)+h_0(F)
558:          \frac{\Gamma(p_i)+h_2(V_{p_i})}{V_{p_i}}.
559: \end{equation}
560: Here \(p_{\rm out}=\P_\nu(G=1)\), which is dyadic. Deterministic
561: inputs are frozen and deleted.
562: \end{proposition}
563: \begin{proof}
579: FEI makes the infimum nonnegative; a single example makes it finite.
580: Output negation gives symmetry, constants give the endpoint values
581: zero, and a dictator gives \(\Gamma_C(1/2)\le C\).
582: 
583: Choose disjoint child functions of the prescribed probabilities
584: whose slacks approach the infima. Their raw entropy composition is
585: \[
586:  \HH(F)=\HH_\nu(G)+
587:     \sum_i\frac{\I_i^\nu(G)}{V_{p_i}}
589: \]
590: This follows either from the same spectral factorization as
591: Lemma~\ref{biasfull:composition}, or by conditioning each child
592: spectrum on being nonempty. Consequently
593: \[
594:  C\I(F)-\HH(F)=
627:                        \frac{\HH(g)}{\I(g)-1}.
628: \end{equation}
629: Indeed, balanced self-composition of a gate with influence \(a>1\)
630: and entropy \(h\) gives ratios tending to \(h/(a-1)\).
631: Conversely, for any nonconstant \(f\), the balanced function
632: \(g=z\prod_{j=1}^r f(x^{(j)})\), with a fresh uniform sign \(z\),
633: satisfies \(\HH(g)=r\HH(f)\) and
634: \(\I(g)-1=r\I(f)\). A balanced function of influence one is a
635: signed dictator and has zero entropy.
636: In particular, whenever FEI holds with \(C\),
637: \(\Gamma_C(1/2)=C\).
638: 
639: The quantifier over every gate in~\eqref{biasfull:gatecondition}
640: already contains unrestricted FEI at uniform input. A proof for a
641: restricted family of gates supplies a composition theorem for that
642: family; it does not remove this universal quantifier.
